(b) Using the method of separation of variables, solve 4𝑢𝑥+ 𝑢𝑦= 3𝑢, given
𝑢(𝑥, 0) = 4𝑒2𝑥.
Solution:
We assume a solution of the form:
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑦) = 𝑋(𝑥)𝑌(𝑦).
Substituting this into the PDE: 4(𝑋′𝑌) + (𝑋𝑌′) = 3(𝑋𝑌)
CO-5; SO-1;   Rearranging: 4𝑋′𝑌+ 𝑋𝑌′ −3𝑋𝑌= 0.
BL- High   Dividing both sides by 𝑋𝑌:   [7]
4𝑋′   𝑌′
𝑋+ 𝑌= 3.
Since the left-hand side is a sum of two functions, each depending on a
separate variable, we set it equal to a constant 𝜆
4𝑋′   𝑌′
𝑋= 𝜆, 𝑌= 3 −𝜆.   (1)
The equation for 𝑋(𝑥) is:
4𝑋′ = 𝜆𝑋.
********** 6 **********
--- page 7 ---
Solving this separable equation:
𝜆
𝑋= 𝐶1𝑒 4𝑥   (1)
The equation for Y(y)Y(y) is:
𝑌′ = (3 −𝜆)𝑌.
𝑌= 𝐶2𝑒(3−𝜆)𝑦   (1)
Thus, the general solution is:
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑦) = 𝐶𝑒(𝜆 4)𝑥𝑒(3−𝜆)𝑦   (1)
Apply Initial Condition
We are given:
𝑢(𝑥, 0) = 4𝑒2𝑥
Substituting y=0
𝐶𝑒(𝜆 4)𝑥= 4𝑒2𝑥.
For this to hold for all xx, we must have:
𝜆 = 2 ⇒𝜆= 8   (1)
4
Thus,
𝐶= 4.   (1)
Substituting 𝜆= 8 into our general solution:
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑦) = 4𝑒2𝑥+ (−5𝑦).
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑦) = 4𝑒2𝑥−5𝑦   (1)

(b) Using the method of separation of variables, solve 4𝑢𝑥+ 𝑢𝑦= 3𝑢, given
𝑢(𝑥, 0) = 4𝑒2𝑥.
Solution:
We assume a solution of the form:
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑦) = 𝑋(𝑥)𝑌(𝑦).

separate variable, we set it equal to a constant 𝜆
4𝑋′   𝑌′
𝑋= 𝜆, 𝑌= 3 −𝜆.   (1)

(b) Using the method of separation of variables, solve 4𝑢𝑥+ 𝑢𝑦= 3𝑢, given
𝑢(𝑥, 0) = 4𝑒2𝑥.
Solution:
We assume a solution of the form:
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑦) = 𝑋(𝑥)𝑌(𝑦).
Substituting this into the PDE: 4(𝑋′𝑌) + (𝑋𝑌′) = 3(𝑋𝑌)
CO-5; SO-1;   Rearranging: 4𝑋′𝑌+ 𝑋𝑌′ −3𝑋𝑌= 0.
BL- High   Dividing both sides by 𝑋𝑌:   [7]
4𝑋′   𝑌′
𝑋+ 𝑌= 3.
Since the left-hand side is a sum of two functions, each depending on a
separate variable, we set it equal to a constant 𝜆
4𝑋′   𝑌′
𝑋= 𝜆, 𝑌= 3 −𝜆.   (1)
The equation for 𝑋(𝑥) is:
4𝑋′ = 𝜆𝑋.

𝜆

𝑌= 𝐶2𝑒(3−𝜆)𝑦   (1)
Thus, the general solution is:
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑦) = 𝐶𝑒(𝜆 4)𝑥𝑒(3−𝜆)𝑦   (1)
Apply Initial Condition
We are given:
𝑢(𝑥, 0) = 4𝑒2𝑥
Substituting y=0
𝐶𝑒(𝜆 4)𝑥= 4𝑒2𝑥.
For this to hold for all xx, we must have:
𝜆 = 2 ⇒𝜆= 8   (1)

𝐶= 4.   (1)
Substituting 𝜆= 8 into our general solution:
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑦) = 4𝑒2𝑥+ (−5𝑦).
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑦) = 4𝑒2𝑥−5𝑦   (1)

(b) Using the method of separation of variables, solve 𝑢𝑥= 2𝑢𝑡+ 𝑢, given
𝑢(𝑥, 0) = 6𝑒−3𝑥
Solution:
We use the method of separation of variables by assuming:
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑡) = 𝑋(𝑥)𝑇(𝑡)   (1)
Substituting this into the given PDE:
CO-5; SO-1;   (𝑋′𝑇) = 2(𝑋𝑇′) + 𝑋𝑇   [7]
BL- High   𝑋′−𝑋   2𝑇′
=
𝑋   𝑇
Since the left-hand side depends only on 𝑥 and the right-hand side
depends only on 𝑡, they must be equal to a constant 𝜆
𝑋′−𝑋   𝑇′   𝜆
𝑋 = 𝜆, 𝑇= 2.
Rearrange:
𝑋′ −𝑋= 𝜆𝑋.
********** 7 **********
--- page 8 ---
𝑋′ = (𝜆+ 1)𝑋.
Solving this first-order ODE:
𝑋= 𝐶1⁡𝑒(𝜆+1)𝑥⁡   (1)
Now,
𝑇′ = 𝜆 𝑇
2
𝑇= 𝐶2𝑒(𝜆 2)𝑡.   (1)
General Solution: Since 𝑢(𝑥, 𝑡) = 𝑋(𝑥)𝑇(𝑡), we get:
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑡) = 𝐶𝑒(𝜆+1)𝑥+𝜆 2𝑡.   (1)
Initial Condition
The given initial condition is: 𝑢(𝑥, 0) = 6𝑒−3𝑥
Substituting 𝑡= 0
𝐶𝑒(𝜆+1)𝑥= 6𝑒−3𝑥
For this to hold for all xx, we must have:
𝜆+ 1 = −3 ⇒𝜆= −4.   (1)
Thus, 𝐶= 6..   (1)
Substituting 𝜆= −4
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑡) = 6𝑒−3𝑥−2𝑡.   (1)
This is the required solution.

(b) Using the method of separation of variables, solve 𝑢𝑥= 2𝑢𝑡+ 𝑢, given
𝑢(𝑥, 0) = 6𝑒−3𝑥
Solution:
We use the method of separation of variables by assuming:
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑡) = 𝑋(𝑥)𝑇(𝑡)   (1)
Substituting this into the given PDE:
CO-5; SO-1;   (𝑋′𝑇) = 2(𝑋𝑇′) + 𝑋𝑇   [7]
BL- High   𝑋′−𝑋   2𝑇′
=
𝑋   𝑇
Since the left-hand side depends only on 𝑥 and the right-hand side
depends only on 𝑡, they must be equal to a constant 𝜆
𝑋′−𝑋   𝑇′   𝜆
𝑋 = 𝜆, 𝑇= 2.
Rearrange:
𝑋′ −𝑋= 𝜆𝑋.
********** 7 **********
--- page 8 ---

Now,
𝑇′ = 𝜆 𝑇
2
𝑇= 𝐶2𝑒(𝜆 2)𝑡.   (1)
General Solution: Since 𝑢(𝑥, 𝑡) = 𝑋(𝑥)𝑇(𝑡), we get:
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑡) = 𝐶𝑒(𝜆+1)𝑥+𝜆 2𝑡.   (1)
Initial Condition
The given initial condition is: 𝑢(𝑥, 0) = 6𝑒−3𝑥
Substituting 𝑡= 0
𝐶𝑒(𝜆+1)𝑥= 6𝑒−3𝑥
For this to hold for all xx, we must have:
𝜆+ 1 = −3 ⇒𝜆= −4.   (1)
Thus, 𝐶= 6..   (1)
Substituting 𝜆= −4
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑡) = 6𝑒−3𝑥−2𝑡.   (1)
This is the required solution.

𝜕2𝑢
= −𝑐2𝑐𝑜𝑠⁡(𝑐𝑡)𝑠𝑖𝑛⁡(𝑥)   (2)
𝜕𝑡2

= −𝑐𝑜𝑠⁡(𝑐𝑡)𝑠𝑖𝑛⁡(𝑥).

Since both sides are equal, the function satisfies the wave equation

(b) Using the method of separation of variables, solve 𝑢𝑥= 2𝑢𝑡+ 𝑢, given
𝑢(𝑥, 0) = 6𝑒−3𝑥
Solution:
We use the method of separation of variables by assuming:
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑡) = 𝑋(𝑥)𝑇(𝑡)   (1)
Substituting this into the given PDE:
CO-5; SO-1;   (𝑋′𝑇) = 2(𝑋𝑇′) + 𝑋𝑇   [7]
BL- High   𝑋′−𝑋   2𝑇′
=
𝑋   𝑇
Since the left-hand side depends only on 𝑥 and the right-hand side
depends only on 𝑡, they must be equal to a constant 𝜆
𝑋′−𝑋   𝑇′   𝜆
𝑋 = 𝜆, 𝑇= 2.
Rearrange:
𝑋′ −𝑋= 𝜆𝑋.
********** 7 **********
--- page 8 ---
𝑋′ = (𝜆+ 1)𝑋.
Solving this first-order ODE:
𝑋= 𝐶1⁡𝑒(𝜆+1)𝑥⁡   (1)
Now,
𝑇′ = 𝜆 𝑇
2
𝑇= 𝐶2𝑒(𝜆 2)𝑡.   (1)
General Solution: Since 𝑢(𝑥, 𝑡) = 𝑋(𝑥)𝑇(𝑡), we get:
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑡) = 𝐶𝑒(𝜆+1)𝑥+𝜆 2𝑡.   (1)
Initial Condition
The given initial condition is: 𝑢(𝑥, 0) = 6𝑒−3𝑥
Substituting 𝑡= 0
𝐶𝑒(𝜆+1)𝑥= 6𝑒−3𝑥
For this to hold for all xx, we must have:
𝜆+ 1 = −3 ⇒𝜆= −4.   (1)
Thus, 𝐶= 6..   (1)
Substituting 𝜆= −4
𝑢(𝑥, 𝑡) = 6𝑒−3𝑥−2𝑡.   (1)
This is the required solution.
